\displaystyle\psi=16C_{\mathbb{A}}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}+8C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}+\big[\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}+1\big]\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}\right)

to conclude that if
T_{*}
is such that

\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\left(\|\mathbf{f}(\cdot,t)\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}+\left(8C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}(\cdot,t)\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+\big[\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}+1\big]\right)\|\mathbf{\mathbf{v}}_{m}(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}\right)dt\\
<\left(512eC_{\mathbb{A}}^{2}C^{2}_{*rn}\right)^{-1},

(4.33)

then

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})}\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})}+\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 245.00037pt\leq\left(16\sqrt{2}C_{\mathbb{A}}{C}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}+\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\right\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{n/2-1}_{\#\sigma}},

(4.34)

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}+\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\leq\left(8\sqrt{2C_{\mathbb{A}}}{C}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}+\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\right\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{n/2-1}_{\#\sigma}}.

(4.35)

Estimates (
4.13
) and (
4.14
) were taken into account in (
4.34
) and (
4.35
).

Taking into account inequality (
4.13
) again, we obtain that condition (
4.33
) is satisfied if
T_{*}
is such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\|\mathbf{f}(\cdot,t)\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}dt+\left(8C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+\big[\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}+1\big]\right)\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\|\mathbf{v}(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}dt<\left(512eC_{\mathbb{A}}^{2}C^{2}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}.

(4.36)

Note that condition (
4.36
) gives condition (
4.28
) with

\displaystyle A_{1}=8C^{2}_{*rn},\quad A_{2}=\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}+1,\quad A_{3}=\left(512eC_{\mathbb{A}}^{2}C^{2}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}.

Inequalities (
4.34
) and (
4.35
) imply that there exists a subsequence of
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
converging weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})
to a function

{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})\cup L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})
.
Then the subsequence converges to
{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}
also weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
.
Since
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
is the subsequence of the sequence that converges weakly in
L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
to the weak solution,
\mathbf{u}
, of problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
) on
\left[0,T_{*}\right]
, we conclude that

\mathbf{u}={\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cup L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
.

This implies that
\mathbf{u}
is a Serrin-type solution on the interval
[0,T_{*}]
and we thus proved item (i) of the theorem.

(ii) Repeating for
\mathbf{u}
the reasoning related to inequality (
4.27
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}\leq C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}\leq C_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1})}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2})},

(4.37)

that is,
({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}
.
By (
1.1
) and (
1.5
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\right\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}\leq\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}({\mathbf{u}})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})^{n\times n}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}

and thus

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2})},

i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-2})}
.
We also have
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
.

Then (
2.9
) implies that

{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-2})}
and hence by Theorem
6.8
we obtain that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})
,
which also means that
\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}}\to 0

as
{t\to 0}
.

To prove the theorem claim about the associated pressure
p
we remark that it satisfies (
2.10
),
where
\mathbf{F}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})

due to the theorem conditions and the inclusion
({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}
.
By Lemma
6.5
for gradient, with
s=n/2-1
, equation (
2.10
) has a unique solution
p
in

L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})
.

(iii) The energy equalities (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) immediately follow from Theorem
3.5
.

(iv) The solution uniqueness follows from Theorem
3.6
.
∎

REMARK 4.2
.

Since
\|\mathbf{f}(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}
is integrable on
(0,T]
by the theorem condition and

\|(K{\mathbf{u}}^{0})(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}
is integrable on
(0,\infty)
by the inequality (
4.5
), we conclude that due to the absolute continuity of the Lebesgue integrals, for arbitrarily large data
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}
there exists
T_{*}>0
such that condition (
4.28
) holds.

Estimating the integrand in the second integral in (
4.28
) according to (
4.5
), we arrive at the following assertion allowing an explicit estimate of
T_{*}
for arbitrarily large data if
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
.

COROLLARY 4.3

(Serrin-type solution for arbitrarily large data but small time or vice versa.)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
T>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2}
.

Then there exist constants
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}>0
that are independent of
{\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}
but may depend on
T
,
n
,
\|\mathbb{A}\|
and
C_{\mathbb{A}}
, such that
if
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfies the inequality

\displaystyle T_{*}\left[\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}^{2}+\left(A_{1}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+A_{2}\right)\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}\right]<A_{3},

(4.38)

then there exists a Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes initial value problem.
This solution satisfies items (ii)-(iv) in Theorem
4.1

Estimating the second integral in (
4.28
) according to (
4.8
), we arrive at the following assertion.

COROLLARY 4.4

(Existence of Serrin-type solution for arbitrary time but small data)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
T>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}
.

Then there exist constants
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}>0
that are independent of
{\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}
but may depend on
T
,
n
,
\|\mathbb{A}\|
and
C_{\mathbb{A}}
, such that
if
{\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}
satisfy the inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}^{2}+\left(A_{1}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+A_{2}\right)\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}<A_{3},

(4.39)

then there exists a Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem.
This solution satisfies items (ii)-(iv) in Theorem
4.1
with
T_{*}=T
.

4.4
Spatial regularity of Serrin-type solutions for constant anisotropic viscosity coefficients

THEOREM 4.5

(Spatial regularity of Serrin-type solution for arbitrarily large data.)
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
r>{n}/{2}-1
, and
T>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
4.28
) from Theorem
4.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Proof.

The existence of the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
is proved in Theorem
4.1
(i), and we will prove that it has a higher smoothness.
We will employ the same Galerkin approximation used in Section
4.2
and in the proof of Theorem
4.1
(i).

Step (a).

Let us estimate the last term in (
4.26
) for the case
n/2-1<r<n/2
.
By (
4.27
) we obtain from (
4.26
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\leq 8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}

(4.40)

implying

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}\leq 8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}.

(4.41)

By Gronwall’s inequality (
6.21
), we obtain from (
4.41
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\leq\exp\left(8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}\right)\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2}\right].

(4.42)

We have
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}\leq\|\mathbf{u}^{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}
and
by (
4.35
), the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}
is bounded.
Then (
4.42
) implies that the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}
is bounded as well.
Integrating (
4.40
), we conclude that

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\leq 32C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\\
+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+32C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2}.

(4.43)

Inequalities (
4.42
) and (
4.43
) mean that the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }n/2-1<r<n/2.

(4.44)

Step (b).

Let now
r=n/2
.
Then by the multiplication Theorem
6.1
(a) and
relation (
1.20
),

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|^{2}_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}=\left\|\nabla\cdot({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{n/2})^{n\times n}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2}}^{2},

(4.45)

where

C_{*rn}=C_{*}(n/2,n/2+1/2,n)
.

Then by (
4.45
) we obtain from (
4.26
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1}}^{2}\leq 8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}^{2},

(4.46)

implying

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}\leq 8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}^{2}\,.

(4.47)

By Gronwall’s inequality (
6.21
), we obtain from (
4.47
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\leq\exp\left(8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2})}\right)\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})}^{2}\right].

(4.48)

We have
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}\leq\|\mathbf{u}^{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}
and
by (
4.44
), the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2})}
is bounded as well.
Then (
4.48
) implies that the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}
is also bounded.
Integrating (
4.46
), we conclude that

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2+1})}\leq 32C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2})}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\\
+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}+32C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})}^{2}\leq C<\infty.

(4.49)

Inequalities (
4.48
) and (
4.49
) mean that the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }r=n/2.

(4.50)

Step(c).

Let now
kn/2<r\leq(k+1)n/2
,
k=1,2,3,\ldots

By the multiplication Theorem
6.1
(a) and
relation (
1.20
),

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot

\displaystyle\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}\leq C_{*rn}^{2}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}^{2}\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1})^{n\times n}}^{2}\leq C_{*rn}^{2}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}},

(4.51)

where

C_{*rn}=C_{*}(r-1,r,n)
.

Then by (
4.51
) we obtain from (
4.26
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\leq 8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}

(4.52)

implying

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}\leq 8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}.

(4.53)

By Gronwall’s inequality (
6.21
), we obtain from (
4.53
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\leq\exp\left(8C_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r})}\right)\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+8C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2}\right],

(4.54)

where
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}\leq\|\mathbf{u}^{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}
.

If
k=1
, then
the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r})}
in (
4.54
) is bounded due to (
4.44
) and (
4.50
).
Then (
4.54
) implies that the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}
is bounded as well.
Integrating (
4.52
), we also conclude that for
k=1
,

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\leq 32C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r})}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\\
+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+32C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2},\quad kn/2<r\leq(k+1)n/2.

(4.55)

Inequalities (
4.54
) and (
4.55
) mean that for
k=1
the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }kn/2<r\leq(k+1)n/2.

(4.56)

If we assume that properties (
4.56
) hold for some integer
k\geq 1
, then by the similar argument, properties (
4.56
) hold with
k
replaced by
k+1
and thus, by induction, they hold for any integer
k
.
Hence, collecting properties (
4.44
), (
4.50
), and (
4.56
) we conclude that
the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }n/2-1<r.

(4.57)

Properties (
4.57
) imply that there exists a subsequence of
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
converging weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
to a function

{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})\cup L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
.
Then the subsequence converges to
{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}
also weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
.
Since
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
is the subsequence of the sequence that converges weakly in
L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
to the weak solution,
\mathbf{u}
, of problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
) on
\left[0,T_{*}\right]
, we conclude that

\mathbf{u}={\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})\cup L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
, for any
r>n/2-1
and we thus finished proving that

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1}).

(4.58)

Step(d).

Repeating for
\mathbf{u}
the reasoning related to inequalities (
4.27
), (
4.45
), (
4.51
), corresponding to the considered
r
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}\leq C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L_{2}(0,T;\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}\leq C_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r})}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1})}

(4.59)

Due to (
4.58
) then
({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}
.
By (
1.1
) and (
1.5
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\right\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1}}\leq\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}({\mathbf{u}})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r})^{n\times n}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}}\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in(0,T),

and thus

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}),F}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1})},

i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})}
.
We also have
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
.
Thus
\mathbf{F}
defined by (
2.8
) belongs to
L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
.
Then (
2.9
) implies that

{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})}
and since
{\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}
, we obtain by Theorem
6.8
that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,
which also means that
\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}\to 0

as
{t\to 0}
.

To prove the theorem claim about the associated pressure
p
, we remark that
p
satisfies (
2.10
).
By Lemma
6.5
for gradient, with
s=r
, equation (
2.10
) has a unique solution
p
in

L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.
∎

As in Corollaries
4.3
and
4.4
, condition (
4.28
) in Theorem
4.5
can be replaced by simpler conditions for particular cases, which leads to the following two assertions.

COROLLARY 4.6

(Serrin-type solution for arbitrarily large data but small time or vice versa.)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
T>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})\cap L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and

\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2}
,
r>{n}/{2}-1
.
Let
T_{*}\in(0,T)
satisfies inequality (
4.38
) in Corollary
4.3
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

COROLLARY 4.7

(Serrin-type solution for arbitrary time but small data)
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
r\geq{n}/{2}-1
, and
T>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let the data
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
satisfy inequality (
4.39
) in Corollary
4.4
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Theorem
4.5
leads also to the following infinite regularity assertion.

COROLLARY 4.8
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
4.28
) from Theorem
4.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
and

p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

Proof.

Taking into account that
\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty}=\bigcap_{r\in{\mathbb{R}}}\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, Theorem
4.5
implies that

{\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

p\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
,

\forall\,r\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Hence
{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.
∎

4.5
Spatial-temporal regularity of Serrin-type solutions for constant anisotropic viscosity coefficients

THEOREM 4.9
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
. Let
r\geq{n}/{2}-1
if
n\geq 3
, while
r>{n}/{2}-1
if
n=2
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
4.28
) from Theorem
4.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})\cup L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})
, while

p\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Proof.

By Theorems
4.1
and
4.5
, we have the inclusions

\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})
and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.
Then we only need to prove the inclusions
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})
and
p\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})
.

Let, first,
n/2-1\leq r<n/2+1
(and also
0<r
if
n=2
).
By relation (
1.20
) and the multiplication Theorem
6.1
(b),
we have

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2}}=\left\|\nabla\cdot({\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{u}})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{u}})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1})^{n\times n}}\leq C^{\prime}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r/2+n/4-1/2}}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}},

where
C^{\prime}_{*rn}=C_{*}(r/2+n/4,r/2+n/4,n)
.

Let now
r\geq n/2+1
. Again, by relation (
1.20
) and by the multiplication Theorem
6.1
(a),
we have

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2}}=\left\|\nabla\cdot({\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{u}})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}\otimes{\mathbf{u}})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1})^{n\times n}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1}}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}^{2},

where
C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}=C_{*}(r-1,r,n)
.
Hence in both cases

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2})}\leq C_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r})}^{2},

(4.60)

where
C_{*rn}
is
C^{\prime}_{*rn}
or
C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}
, respectively.

By (
1.1
) and (
1.5
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2}}\leq\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}({\mathbf{u}})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1})^{n\times n}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r})},

i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})}
.
We also have
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})
.

Then (
2.9
) implies that

{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})}
, while (
2.10
) and Lemma
6.5
for gradient, with
s=r-1
, imply that
p\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})
.
∎

THEOREM 4.10
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
.
Let
r>{n}/{2}-1
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
k\in[1,r+1)
be an integer.
Let

{\mathbf{f}}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})
,
l=0,1,\ldots,k-1
,
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
4.28
) from Theorem
4.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k
, while

p^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k-1
.

Proof.

Some parts of the following proof are inspired by
[
34
, Theorem 3.1]
and
[
36
, Chapter 3, Section 3.6]
, see also
[
27
, Section 7.2]
.

We will employ the mathematical induction argument in the proof.
We first remark that by Theorems
4.1
,
4.5
and
4.9
, if

{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
, then

\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})
,
while
p\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.
This means that the theorem holds true for
k=1
.

Let us assume that the theorem holds true for some
k^{\prime}=k-1\in[1,r)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2l}),\quad l=0,\ldots,k-1,

(4.61)

and prove that it hold also for
l=k
.
To this end, let us differentiate equation (
2.9
)
k-1
times in
t
(in the distribution sense) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{(k)}=\mathbb{P}_{\sigma}\mathbf{F}^{(k-1)}\quad\mbox{in }{\mathbb{T}}\times(0,T),

(4.62)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{(l)}:=\mathbf{f}^{(l)}+\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbf{u}}-\partial_{t}^{l}[({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}]\quad\forall\,l\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(4.63)

Let us denote

s_{l1}:=r-2\max\{(k-1-l),l\},\ s_{l2}:=r-2\min\{(k-1-l),l\}.

Then

\displaystyle s_{l1}\leq r-k+1\leq s_{l2},

\displaystyle s_{l1}+s_{l2}=r-2(k-1-l)+r-2l=2(r-k+1),\quad\forall\ l=0,\ldots,k-1.

The theorem condition
r-k+1>0
implies
s_{l1}+s_{l2}>0
.

Step 1: Convection term

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k-1}[({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}]=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}C_{k-1}^{l}({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}

(4.64)

where
C_{k-1}^{l}
are the binomial coefficients.

Case (A).
Let
0\leq l\leq(k-1)/2
. Then
s_{l1}=r-2(k-1-l)
,
s_{l2}=r-2l
.

Subcase (A1).
Let
{n}/{2}-1<r\leq n/2+2l
. Then
s_{l2}=r-2l\leq n/2
.
By the theorem conditions,
r+1-n/2>0
and
r-k+1>0
, and hence there exists
\epsilon\in(0,\min\{r+1-n/2,2(r-k+1)\})
and thus

(r+1-n/2)-\epsilon>0,\quad s_{l1}-\epsilon/2\leq s_{l2}-\epsilon/2<n/2,\quad s_{l1}+s_{l2}-\epsilon=2(r-k+1)-\epsilon>0.

By relation (
1.20
) and the multiplication Theorem
6.1
(b),
we have

\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}}=\left\|\nabla\cdot({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}}\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k+(r+1-n/2)-\epsilon})^{n\times n}}=\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{s_{l1}+s_{l2}-\epsilon-n/2})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq C^{\prime}_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{s_{l1}-\epsilon/2}}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{s_{l2}-\epsilon/2}}\leq C^{\prime}_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2l}},

\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k}}\leq\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k+(r+1-n/2)-\epsilon}}=\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{s_{l1}+s_{l2}-\epsilon-n/2}}\\
\leq C^{\prime}_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{s_{l1}-\epsilon/2}}\left\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{(H_{\#}^{s_{l2}-\epsilon/2})^{n\times n}}\leq C^{\prime}_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2l}},

where
C^{\prime}_{*rn}=C_{*}(s_{l1}-\epsilon/2,s_{l2}-\epsilon/2,n)
.

Subcase (A2).
Let
r>n/2+2l
. Then
s_{l2}=r-2l>n/2
.

Hence by relation (
1.20
) and the multiplication Theorem
6.1
(a),
we have

\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}}=\left\|\nabla\cdot({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}}\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)})^{n\times n}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2l}},

\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k}}\leq\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\\
\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\left\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2l}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2l}},

where
C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}=C_{*}(s_{l1},s_{l2},n)
.

Thus, combining the cases (A1) and (A2), we obtain that for any
r>n/2-1
and for
0\leq l\leq(k-1)/2
,

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}}\leq C_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2l}},

(4.65)

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k}}\leq C_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)}}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2l}},

(4.66)

where
C_{*rn}
is
C^{\prime}_{*rn}
or
C^{\prime\prime}_{*rn}
, respectively.

Case (B).
Let
(k-1)/2\leq l\leq k-1
. Then taking into account that

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}}=\left\|\nabla\cdot({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\otimes{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}}=\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k}},

we arrive at the case (A) for
l^{\prime}=k-1-l
and finally to the same estimates (
4.65
)–(
4.66
).

Thus for any
r>n/2-1
and any integer
l\in[0,k-1]
, we obtain the estimates

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k})}\leq C_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)})}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2l})},

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})}\leq C_{*rn}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1-l)}\right\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2(k-1-l)})}\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{(l)}\right\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2l})},

Hence by (
4.64
) and (
4.61
),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k-1}[({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}]\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k}).

(4.67)

Step 2: Linear terms and right-hand side

Due to (
4.61
),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{l}\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbf{u}}=\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbf{u}}^{(k-1)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k}).

(4.68)

We also have
{\mathbf{f}}^{(k-1)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2k})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})
.
Then (
4.63
), (
4.67
) and (
4.68
)
imply that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}^{(k-1)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2k})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1-2k}).

(4.69)

Thus by (
4.62
),

{\mathbf{u}}^{(k)}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k})}\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})
.

Step 3: Pressure

The associated pressure
p
satisfies (
2.10
). Differentiating it in time, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla p^{(l)}=\mathbb{P}_{g}\mathbf{F}^{(l)}\quad\quad\mbox{in }{\mathbb{T}}\times(0,T),\quad l=0,1,\ldots,k-1.

(4.70)

By the same reasoning as in the proof of (
4.69
), the similar inclusions for junior derivatives also hold,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1-2l}),\quad l=0,1,\ldots,k-1.

By Lemma
6.5
for gradient, with
s=r-1-2l
and
s=r-2l
, respectively, equation (
4.70
) implies that
p^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-2l})
.
∎

COROLLARY 4.11
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
,
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
4.28
) from Theorem
4.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

p\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

Proof.

Taking into account that
\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty}=\bigcap_{r\in{\mathbb{R}}}\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, Theorem
4.10
implies that for any integer
k\geq 0
,

{\mathbf{u}}^{(k)}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k})}\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})
,

p^{(k)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1-2k})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k})
, for any
r\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Hence
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

p\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.
∎

4.6
Regularity of two-dimensional weak solution for constant viscosity coefficients

The regularity results of Section
4.4
and
4.5
hold for
n=2
, but as for the the isotropic constant-coefficient case (cf. e.g.,
[
36
, Chapter 3, Sections 3.3, 3.5.1]
,
[
27
, Section 6.5]
) these results can be essentially improved for
n=2
also in the anisotropic setting with constant coefficients.

Let us give a counterpart of Theorem
4.5
that for
n=2
is valid on any time interval
[0,T]
(and not only on its special subinterval
[0,T_{*}]
).

THEOREM 4.12

(Spatial regularity of solution for arbitrarily large data.)
.

Let
n=2
,
r>0
, and
T>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Proof.

The proof coincides word-for-word with the proof of Theorem
4.5
if we take there
n=2
while replacing
T_{*}
by
T
and the reference to (
4.35
) for the boundedness of the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}
for
n=2
by the reference to the corresponding inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{1})}\leq 4C_{\mathbb{A}}\left(\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{L}_{2\#}}+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{-1})}\right).

obtained as inequality (59) in our paper
[
24
]
.
∎

The following assertion can be proved similarly to Corollary
4.8
.

COROLLARY 4.13
.

Let
T>0
and
n=2
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
is such that

{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
and

p\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

The next three assertions on spatial-temporal regularity for
n=2
are the corresponding counterparts of Theorems
4.9
,
4.10
and Corollary
4.11
and are proved in a similar way after replacing there
T_{*}
by
T
.

THEOREM 4.14
.

Let
T>0
,
n\geq 2
and
r>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})\cup L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})
,

p\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

THEOREM 4.15
.

Let
T>0
,
n=2
and
r>0
.
Let the coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}
be constant the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
k\in[1,r+1)
be an integer.
Let

{\mathbf{f}}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})
,
l=0,1,\ldots,k-1
,
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k
;

p^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k-1
.

.

Let
T>0
and

.

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}

) hold.
