{\mathbf{f}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
,
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
is such that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

p\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

5
Serrin-type solution existence and regularity for variable anisotropic viscosity coefficients

In this section we generalise to the anisotropic variable viscosity coefficients the analysis of the existence and regularity of Serrin-type solutions for any
n\geq 2
given in Section
4
for the anisotropic constant viscosity coefficients.

5.1
Preliminary results
for variable anisotropic viscosity coefficients

For some
n\geq 2
,
r\geq{n}/{2}-1
, and
T>0
, let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>\frac{n}{2}+\max\{|r-1|,|n/2-2|\}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let also
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
.

We employ the Galerkin approximation as in Section
4.2
and repeating the same arguments arrive at the same equations (
4.17
) but now with the variable coefficients
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{x},t)
.
These equations can be now re-written as

\langle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m},\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\mathbf{w}_{k}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}}+\left\langle a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{j\beta}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}),E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}{\mathbf{w}}_{k})\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}+\langle\Lambda^{r-1}_{\#}[({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}],\Lambda^{r+1}_{\#}{\bf w}_{k}\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\\
\hskip 10.00002pt=\langle\Lambda^{r-1}_{\#}\mathbf{f},\Lambda^{r+1}_{\#}\mathbf{w}_{k}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}}+\langle{\nabla\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\mathbf{v}}_{m},\nabla\Lambda^{r}_{\#}{\bf w}_{k}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}}-\left\langle E_{j\beta}({\bf v}_{m}),a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}{\mathbf{w}}_{k})\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\\
-\left\langle E_{j\beta}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}),a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}{\mathbf{w}}_{k})-\Lambda^{r}_{\#}[a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}{\mathbf{w}}_{k})]\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}},\quad\forall\,k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}.

(5.1)

Multiplying equations in (
5.1
) by
\widetilde{\eta}_{k,m}(t)
and summing them up over
k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, we obtain

\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\left\|\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{0}_{\#}}^{2}+a_{{\mathbb{T}}}(t;\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})=\langle\Lambda^{r-1}_{\#}\mathbf{f},\Lambda^{r+1}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}}+\langle{\nabla\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\mathbf{v}}_{m},\nabla\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}}\\
-\left\langle E_{j\beta}({\bf v}_{m}),a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}-\left\langle E_{j\beta}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}),a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})-\Lambda^{r}_{\#}[a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})]\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\\
-\langle\Lambda^{r-1}_{\#}[({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}],\Lambda^{r+1}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}.

(5.2)

From (
2.7
) we have

\displaystyle a_{{\mathbb{T}}}(t;\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m},\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1}}^{2}=\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1}}.

(5.3)

Let us now estimate the terms in the right hand side of (
5.2
).
For the first two terms and for the last one, estimates (
4.21
), (
4.22
) and (
4.24
) still hold.

Further, since
\tilde{\sigma}+1>n/2
, we obtain by Theorem
6.1
(a), relation (
1.2
), and inequality (
1.20
),

\left|\left\langle E_{j\beta}({\bf v}_{m}),a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\right|\leq\|E_{j\beta}({\mathbf{v}}_{m})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r})^{n\times n}}\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{-r})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq\|E_{j\beta}({\mathbf{v}}_{m})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r})^{n\times n}}C_{*\tilde{\sigma}rn}\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}}\|\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{-r})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq C_{*\tilde{\sigma}rn}\|\mathbb{A}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\,\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}},

(5.4)

where

C_{*\tilde{\sigma}rn}:=C_{*}(-r,\tilde{\sigma}+1,n)
,

\|\mathbb{A}(\cdot,t)\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}:=\left|\left\{\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,t)\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}}\right\}_{\alpha,\beta,i,j=1}^{n}\right|_{F}
.

By Theorem
6.3
with
\theta=r
,
s=0
,

\left|\left\langle E_{j\beta}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}),a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})-\Lambda^{r}_{\#}[a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})]\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\right|\\
\leq\|E_{j\beta}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1})^{n\times n}}\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\Lambda^{r}_{\#}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})-\Lambda^{r}_{\#}[a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})]\|_{({H}_{\#}^{-r+1})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}\|E_{j\beta}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1})^{n\times n}}|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}}\|E_{i\alpha}(\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m})\|_{(H_{\#}^{0}))^{n\times n}}\leq\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}|\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}},

(5.5)

where

|\mathbb{A}(\cdot,t)|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}:=\left|\left\{|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,t)|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}}\right\}_{\alpha,\beta,i,j=1}^{n}\right|_{F}\leq\|\mathbb{A}(\cdot,t)\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}
.

Implementing estimates (
4.21
), (
4.22
), (
4.24
), (
5.3
), (
5.4
), (
5.5
) in (
5.2
) and using Young’s inequality, we obtain

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\leq 2\Big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}+\big[C_{*\tilde{\sigma}rn}\|\mathbb{A}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+1\big]\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}}\\
+\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}|\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+\left\|({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}\Big)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}\\
\leq 4C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}+\big[C_{*\tilde{\sigma}rn}\|\mathbb{A}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+1\big]\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}}\\
+\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}|\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+\left\|({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}\Big)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}.

Hence by the inequality
(\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i})^{2}\leq k\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}^{2}
(following from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality),

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\leq 20C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}+\big[C_{*\tilde{\sigma}rn}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+1\big]\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\\
+\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+\left\|({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}\Big).

(5.6)

Note that by the similar reasoning, but without employing in (
4.9
)–(
4.10
) the function
\mathbf{v}
, we obtain that
\mathbf{u}_{m}
satisfies the differential inequality

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\leq 12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}+\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+\left\|({\mathbf{u}}_{m}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}\Big).

(5.7)

5.2
Serrin-type solution existence for variable anisotropic viscosity coefficients

Employing the results from Section
5.1
for
r=n/2-1
, we are now in the position to prove the existence of Serrin-type solutions.

THEOREM 5.1
.

Let
n\geq 2
, and
T>0
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>\frac{n}{2}+|n/2-2|
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}
.

(i) Then there exist constants
A_{1}\geq 0
,
A_{2}\geq 0
and
A_{3}>0
that are independent of
{\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}
but may depend on
T
,
n
,
\|\mathbb{A}\|
and
C_{\mathbb{A}}
, such that
if
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\|\mathbf{f}(\cdot,t)\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}dt+\left(A_{1}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+A_{2}\right)\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\|(K{\mathbf{u}}^{0})(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}dt<A_{3},

(5.8)

where
K
is the operator defined in (
4.3
),
then there exists a solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
) in

L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
, which is thus a Serrin-type solution.

(ii) In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-2}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}}=0,

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})
.

(iii) Moreover,
\mathbf{u}
satisfies the following energy equality for any
[t_{0},t]\subset[0,T_{*}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,t)\|_{\mathbf{L}_{2\#}}^{2}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}a_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\tau;{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau),{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau))d\tau=\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,t_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbf{L}_{2\#}}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\langle\mathbf{f}(\cdot,\tau),{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}d\tau.

(5.9)

It particularly implies the standard energy equality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\ {\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,t)\|_{\mathbf{L}_{2\#}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}a_{{\mathbb{T}}}(\tau;{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau),{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau))d\tau=\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{L}_{2\#}}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathbf{f}(\cdot,\tau),{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\,d\tau\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T_{*}].

(5.10)

(iv) The solution
\mathbf{u}
is unique in the class of solutions from

L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})

satisfying the energy inequality (
2.19
) on the interval
[0,T_{*}]
.

Proof.

(i)
Let
r=n/2-1
.
The estimate (
4.27
) still holds.
Let us fix any small
\tilde{\sigma}_{n}
such that
\tilde{\sigma}\geq\tilde{\sigma}_{n}>{n}/{2}+|n/2-2|=\max\{2,n-2\}
.
Then by (
4.31
) we obtain from (
5.6
),

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2-1}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}\leq\left(160C^{2}_{*rn}C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}+20C_{\mathbb{A}}\bar{C}_{n}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1},F}\right)\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}\\
+20C_{\mathbb{A}}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}+8C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}+\big[\widetilde{C}_{*n}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1},F}+1\big]\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}\right),

(5.11)

where

\bar{C}_{n}:=\bar{C}_{0,n/2-1,\tilde{\sigma}_{n}}
,
\widetilde{C}_{*n}:=C_{*\tilde{\sigma}_{n},n/2-1,n}=C_{*}(-n/2+1,\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1,n)
.

Let us now apply to (
5.11
) Lemma
6.12
with

\displaystyle\eta=\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2-1}_{\#}}^{2},\ \eta_{0}=0,\ y=\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2},\ b=\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1},\ c=160C^{2}_{*rn}C_{\mathbb{A}},\ \phi=20C_{\mathbb{A}}\bar{C}_{n}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1},F}

\displaystyle\psi=20C_{\mathbb{A}}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}+8C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}+\big[\widetilde{C}_{*n}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1},F}+1\big]\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}\right)

to conclude that if
T_{*}
is such that

\int_{0}^{T_{*}}e^{\Phi(T_{*})-\Phi(t)}\left(\|\mathbf{f}(\cdot,t)\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}+\left(8C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}(\cdot,t)\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+\big[\widetilde{C}_{*n}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1},F}+1\big]\right)\|\mathbf{\mathbf{v}}_{m}(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}\right)dt\\
<\left(640eC_{\mathbb{A}}^{2}C^{2}_{*rn}\right)^{-1},

(5.12)

where

\Phi(s):=\int_{0}^{s}\phi(\tau)d\tau=20C_{\mathbb{A}}\bar{C}_{n}^{2}\int_{0}^{s}|\mathbb{A}(\cdot,\tau)|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1},F}d\tau,

then

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})}\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})}+\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})}\leq\left(8\sqrt{10}C_{\mathbb{A}}{C}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}+\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\right\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{n/2-1}_{\#\sigma}},

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}+\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\leq\left(4\sqrt{10C_{\mathbb{A}}}{C}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}+\left\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\right\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}^{n/2-1}_{\#\sigma}}.

(5.14)

Estimates (
4.13
) and (
4.14
) were taken into account in (
5.13
) and (
5.14
).

Taking into account inequality (
4.13
), we obtain that condition (
5.12
) is satisfied if
T_{*}
is such that

\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\|\mathbf{f}(\cdot,t)\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}dt+\left(8C^{2}_{*rn}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+\big[\widetilde{C}_{*n}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T;H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1}),F}+1\big]\right)\int_{0}^{T_{*}}\|\mathbf{v}(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}dt\\
<\left(640C_{\mathbb{A}}^{2}C^{2}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}\exp\left(-1-20C_{\mathbb{A}}\bar{C}_{n}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T;H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1}),F}\,T\right).

(5.15)

Note that condition (
5.15
) gives condition (
5.8
) with

\displaystyle A_{1}=8C^{2}_{*rn},\quad A_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{*n}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T;H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{n}+1}),F}+1,

\displaystyle A_{3}=\left(640C_{\mathbb{A}}^{2}C^{2}_{*rn}\right)^{-1}\exp\left(-1-20C_{\mathbb{A}}\bar{C}_{n}^{2}\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T;H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}),F}\,T\right).

Inequalities (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) imply that there exists a subsequence of
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
converging weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})
to a function

{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})\cup L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})
.
Then the subsequence converges to
{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}
also weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
.
Since
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
is the subsequence of the sequence that converges weakly in
L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
to the weak solution,
\mathbf{u}
, of problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
) on
\left[0,T_{*}\right]
, we conclude that

\mathbf{u}={\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cup L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
.

This implies that
\mathbf{u}
is a Serrin-type solution on the interval
[0,T_{*}]
and we thus proved item (i) of the theorem.

(ii) As in step (ii) of the proof of Theorem
5.1
, estimate (
4.37
) implies that

({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}
.
By (
1.1
) and (
1.5
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\right\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}\leq\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}({\mathbf{u}})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})^{n\times n}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}

and thus

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}),F}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2})},

i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-2})}
.
We also have
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
.

Then (
2.9
) implies that

{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-2})}
and hence by Theorem
6.8
we obtain that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})
,
which also means that
\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}}\to 0

as
{t\to 0}
.

To prove the theorem claim about the associated pressure
p
we remark that it satisfies (
2.10
),
where
\mathbf{F}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})

due to the theorem conditions and the inclusion
({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}
.
By Lemma
6.5
for gradient, with
s=n/2-1
, equation (
2.10
) has a unique solution
p
in

L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})
.

(iii) The energy equalities (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) immediately follow from Theorem
3.5
.

(iv) The solution uniqueness follows from Theorem
3.6
.
∎

REMARK 5.2
.

Note that by the Sobolev embedding theorem, the condition
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,
\tilde{\sigma}>|n/2-2|+\frac{n}{2}
, in Theorem
5.1
and further on implies
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];\mathcal{C}_{\#}^{0})\subset L_{\infty}([0,T];L_{\infty\#})
.

REMARK 5.3
.

Since
\|\mathbf{f}(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2}}^{2}
is integrable on
(0,T]
by the theorem condition and

\|(K{\mathbf{u}}^{0})(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}^{2}
is integrable on
(0,\infty)
by the inequality (
4.5
), we conclude that due to the absolute continuity of the Lebesgue integrals, for arbitrarily large data
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}
there exists
T_{*}>0
such that condition (
5.8
) holds.

Estimating the integrand in the second integral in (
5.8
) according to (
4.5
), we arrive at the following assertion allowing an explicit estimate of
T_{*}
for arbitrarily large data if
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
.

COROLLARY 5.4

(Serrin-type solution for arbitrarily large data but small time or vice versa.)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
T>0
.
Let
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>|n/2-2|+\frac{n}{2}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2}
.

Then there exist constants
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}>0
that are independent of
{\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}
but may depend on
T
,
n
,
\|\mathbb{A}\|
and
C_{\mathbb{A}}
, such that
if
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfies the inequality

\displaystyle T_{*}\left[\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}^{2}+\left(A_{1}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+A_{2}\right)\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}\right]<A_{3},

(5.16)

then there exists a Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes initial value problem.
This solution satisfies items (ii)-(iv) in Theorem
5.1

Estimating the second integral in (
5.8
) according to (
4.8
), we arrive at the following assertion.

COROLLARY 5.5

(Existence of Serrin-type solution for arbitrary time but small data)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
T>0
.
Let
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>|n/2-2|+\frac{n}{2}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1}
.

Then there exist constants
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}>0
that are independent of
{\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}
but may depend on
T
,
n
,
\|\mathbb{A}\|
and
C_{\mathbb{A}}
, such that
if
{\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}
satisfy the inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})}^{2}+\left(A_{1}\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}+A_{2}\right)\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}<A_{3},

(5.17)

then there exists a Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem.
This solution satisfies items (ii)-(iv) in Theorem
5.1
with
T_{*}=T
.

5.3
Spatial regularity of Serrin-type solutions for variable anisotropic viscosity coefficients

THEOREM 5.6

(Spatial regularity of Serrin-type solution for arbitrarily large data.)
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
r>{n}/{2}-1
, and
T>0
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>\frac{n}{2}+\max\{|r-1|,|n/2-2|\}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
5.8
) from Theorem
5.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Proof.

The existence of the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})
is proved in Theorem
5.1
(i), and we will prove that it has a higher smoothness.
We will employ the same Galerkin approximation used in Section
5.1
and in the proof of Theorem
5.1
(i).

Step (a).

Let us estimate the last term in (
5.7
) for the case
n/2-1<r<n/2
.
By (
4.27
) we obtain from (
5.7
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\leq 12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}

(5.18)

implying

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}\leq 12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}.

(5.19)

By Gronwall’s inequality (
6.21
), we obtain from (
5.19
) that

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\leq\exp\Big[12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}T_{*}\||\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*})}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}\Big)\Big]\\
\times\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2}\right].

(5.20)

We have
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}\leq\|\mathbf{u}^{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}
and
by (
5.14
), the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}
is bounded.
Then (
5.20
) implies that the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}
is bounded as well.
Integrating (
5.18
), we conclude that

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\leq 48C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}T_{*}\||\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*})}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\\
+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+48C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2}.

(5.21)

Inequalities (
5.20
) and (
5.21
) mean that the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }n/2-1<r<n/2.

(5.22)

Step (b).

Let now
r=n/2
.
By (
4.45
) we obtain from (
5.7
),

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1}}^{2}\leq 12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2}}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}\\
+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}^{2},

(5.23)

implying

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}\leq 12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2}}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2}}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1}}^{2}\,.

(5.24)

By Gronwall’s inequality (
6.21
), we obtain from (
5.24
) that

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\leq\exp\Big[12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}T_{*}\||\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*})}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2})}\Big)\Big]\\
\times\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})}^{2}\right].

(5.25)

We have
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}\leq\|\mathbf{u}^{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}
and
by (
5.22
), the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2})}
is bounded as well.
Then (
5.25
) implies that the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}
is also bounded.
Integrating (
5.23
), we conclude that

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2+1})}\leq 48C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}T_{*}\||\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*})}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2+1/2})}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2})}\\
+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{n/2}_{\#}}^{2}+48C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2-1})}^{2}\leq C<\infty.

(5.26)

Inequalities (
5.25
) and (
5.26
) mean that the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }r=n/2.

(5.27)

Step(c).

Let now
kn/2<r\leq(k+1)n/2
,
k=1,2,3,\ldots

By (
4.51
) we obtain from (
5.7
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\mathbb{A}}^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1}}^{2}\leq 12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}

(5.28)

implying

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}\leq 12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}|\mathbb{A}|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1}}^{2}.

(5.29)

By Gronwall’s inequality (
6.21
), we obtain from (
5.29
) that

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\leq\exp\Big[12C_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}T_{*}\||\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*})}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r})}\Big)\Big]\\
\times\left[\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+12C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2}\right],

(5.30)

where
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}\leq\|\mathbf{u}^{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}
.

If
k=1
, then
the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r})}
in (
5.30
) is bounded due to (
5.22
) and (
5.27
).
Then (
5.30
) implies that the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}
is bounded as well.
Integrating (
5.28
), we also conclude that for
k=1
,

\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\leq 48C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\Big(\bar{C}_{0,r,\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}T_{*}\||\mathbb{A}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*})}+C^{2}_{*rn}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r})}\Big)\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\\
+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{u}_{m}(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{r}_{\#}}^{2}+48C^{2}_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}^{2},\quad kn/2<r\leq(k+1)n/2.

(5.31)

Inequalities (
5.30
) and (
5.31
) mean that for
k=1
the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }kn/2<r\leq(k+1)n/2.

(5.32)

If we assume that properties (
5.32
) hold for some integer
k\geq 1
, then by the similar argument, properties (
5.32
) hold with
k
replaced by
k+1
and thus, by induction, they hold for any integer
k
.
Hence, collecting properties (
5.22
), (
5.27
), and (
5.32
) we conclude that
the sequences

\displaystyle\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ and }\{\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\text{ are bounded for }n/2-1<r.

(5.33)

Properties (
5.33
) imply that there exists a subsequence of
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
converging weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
to a function

{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})\cup L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
.
Then the subsequence converges to
{\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}
also weakly in
L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
.
Since
\{\mathbf{u}_{m}\}
is the subsequence of the sequence that converges weakly in
L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{1})
and weakly-star in
L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0})
to the weak solution,
\mathbf{u}
, of problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
) on
\left[0,T_{*}\right]
, we conclude that

\mathbf{u}={\mathbf{u}}^{\dagger}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})\cup L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
, for any
r>n/2-1
and we thus finished proving and we thus finished proving that

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1}).

(5.34)

Step(d).

Estimate (
4.59
) and inclusion (
5.34
) imply that

({\mathbf{u}}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-1})}
.
By (
1.1
) and (
1.5
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\right\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1}}\leq\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}({\mathbf{u}})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r})^{n\times n}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1}}\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in(0,T),

and thus

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbb{A}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}),F}\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1})},

i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})}
.
We also have
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
.
Thus
\mathbf{F}
defined by (
2.8
) belongs to
L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
.
Then (
2.9
) implies that

{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})}
and since
{\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})}
, we obtain by Theorem
6.8
that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,
which also means that
\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}\to 0

as
{t\to 0}
.

To prove the theorem claim about the associated pressure
p
, we remark that
p
satisfies (
2.10
).
By Lemma
6.5
for gradient, with
s=r
, equation (
2.10
) has a unique solution
p
in

L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.
∎

As in Corollaries
5.4
and
5.5
, condition (
5.8
) in Theorem
5.6
can be replaced by simpler conditions for particular cases, which leads to the following two assertions.

COROLLARY 5.7

(Serrin-type solution for arbitrarily large data but small time or vice versa.)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
T>0
.
Let
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>|r-1|+\frac{n}{2}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})\cap L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{n/2-2})
and

\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}\cap\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{n/2}
,
r>{n}/{2}-1
.
Let
T_{*}\in(0,T)
satisfies inequality (
5.16
) in Corollary
5.4
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

COROLLARY 5.8

(Serrin-type solution for arbitrary time but small data)
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
r\geq{n}/{2}-1
, and
T>0
.
Let
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>|r-1|+\frac{n}{2}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition hold.
Let the data
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
satisfy inequality (
5.17
) in Corollary
5.5
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Theorem
5.6
leads also to the following infinite regularity assertion.

COROLLARY 5.9
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}([0,T];C^{\infty}_{\#})
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
5.8
) from Theorem
5.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
and

p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

Proof.

Taking into account that
\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty}=\bigcap_{r\in{\mathbb{R}}}\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, Theorem
5.6
implies that

{\mathbf{u}}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})}\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

p\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
,

\forall\,r\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Hence
{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T_{*}];\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.
∎

5.4
Spatial-temporal regularity of Serrin-type solutions for variable anisotropic viscosity coefficients

THEOREM 5.10
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
. Let
r\geq{n}/{2}-1
if
n\geq 3
, while
r>{n}/{2}-1
if
n=2
.
Let
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>|r-1|+\frac{n}{2}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
5.8
) from Theorem
5.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})\cup L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})
, while

p\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Proof.

By Theorems
5.1
and
5.6
, we have the inclusions

\mathbf{u}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})
and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.
Then we only need to prove the inclusions
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})
and
p\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})
.

As in the proof of Theorem
5.10
we arrive at estimate (
4.60
).

By (
1.1
), (
1.5
) and the multiplication Theorem
6.1
(a), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2}}\leq\|a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}E_{i\alpha}({\mathbf{u}})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1})^{n\times n}}\leq C_{*}(r-1,\tilde{\sigma}+1,n)\|\mathbb{A}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1},F}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r}},

where we took into account that
\tilde{\sigma}+1>n/2
and
\tilde{\sigma}+1>r
.
Thus

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{*}(r-1,\tilde{\sigma}+1,n)\|\mathbb{A}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}),F}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r})},

i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}\mathbf{u}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})}
.
We also have
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})
.

Then (
2.9
) implies that

{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})}
, while (
2.10
) and Lemma
6.5
for gradient, with
s=r-1
, imply that
p\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})
.
∎

To simplify the following two assertions we assume there that the viscosity coefficients are infinitely smooth it time and space coordinates. This smoothness condition can be relaxed if we instead assume that all the norms of these coefficients encountered in the proof are bounded.

THEOREM 5.11
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
.
Let
r>{n}/{2}-1
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}([0,T];C^{\infty}_{\#})
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
k\in[1,r+1)
be an integer.
Let

{\mathbf{f}}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})
,
l=0,1,\ldots,k-1
,
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
5.8
) from Theorem
5.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k
, while

p^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k-1
.

Proof.

The proof coincide with the proof of the corresponding constant-coefficient Theorem (
4.10
), except the proof of inclusion (
4.68
) in Step 2, that for the variable coefficients is replaced by the following argument.

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k-1}\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbf{u}}=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}C_{k-1}^{l}\nabla\cdot[{\mathbb{A}}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})].

(5.35)

By (
1.1
), (
1.5
) and Theorem
6.1
(a), we have

\|\nabla\cdot[\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})]\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})^{n\times n}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq C_{*l1}\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{l1}},F}\|\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})^{n\times n}}\leq C_{*l1}\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{l1}},F}\|\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2l}},

where
\tilde{\sigma}_{l1}>\max\{n/2,2l+1-r\}
,
C_{*l1}=C_{*}(r-1-2l,\tilde{\sigma}_{l1},n)
.
Thus

\displaystyle\|\nabla\cdot[\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})]\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{*l1}\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{l1}}),F}\|\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2l})},

i.e., due to (
4.61
),

\nabla\cdot[\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})]\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k})},\quad l=0,\ldots,k-1.

On the other hand, by (
1.1
), (
1.5
) and Theorem
6.1
(a), we have

\|\nabla\cdot[\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})]\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2k}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r+2-2k})^{n\times n}}\leq\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-2l})^{n\times n}}\\
\leq C_{*l2}\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{l2}},F}\|\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})\|_{({H}_{\#}^{r-2l})^{n\times n}}\leq C_{*l2}\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{l2}},F}\|\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1-2l}},

where
\tilde{\sigma}_{l2}>\max\{n/2,2l-r\}
,
C_{*l2}=C_{*}(r-2l,\tilde{\sigma}_{l2},n)
.
Thus

\displaystyle\|\nabla\cdot[\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})]\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{*l2}\|\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\|_{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{l2}}),F}\|\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1-2l})},

i.e., due to (
4.61
),

\nabla\cdot[\mathbb{A}^{(k-1-l)}\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{u}}^{(l)})]\in{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2k})},\quad l=0,\ldots,k-1.

Hence by (
5.35
),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k-1}\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbf{u}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r-2k})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{r+1-2k}).

(5.36)

∎

COROLLARY 5.12
.

Let
T>0
and
n\geq 2
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}([0,T];C^{\infty}_{\#})
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
,
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty}
, while
{\mathbf{f}}
,
\mathbf{u}^{0}
and
T_{*}\in(0,T]
satisfy inequality (
5.8
) from Theorem
5.1
.

Then the Serrin-type solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
is such that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

p\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

Proof.

Taking into account that
\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty}=\bigcap_{r\in{\mathbb{R}}}\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, Theorem
5.11
implies that for any integer
k\geq 0
,

{\mathbf{u}}^{(k)}\in{L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k})}\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r+1-2k})
,

p^{(k)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1-2k})\cap L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2k})
, for any
r\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Hence
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

p\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T_{*};\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.
∎

5.5
Regularity of two-dimensional weak solution for variable anisotropic viscosity coefficients

Here we provide a counterpart of Section
4.6
generalised to variable viscosity coefficients.

THEOREM 5.13

(Spatial regularity of solution for arbitrarily large data.)
.

Let
n=2
,
r>0
, and
T>0
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>1+\max\{|r-1|,1\}
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
belongs to

L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1})
.
In addition,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1}),

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r})

\lim_{t\to 0}\|\mathbf{u}(\cdot,t)-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=0

and
p\in L_{2}(0,T_{*};\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

Proof.

The proof coincides word-for-word with the proof of Theorem
5.6
if we take there
n=2
while replacing
T_{*}
by
T
and the reference to (
5.14
) for the boundedness of the sequence
\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|_{L_{2}(0,T_{*};\mathbf{H}_{\#}^{n/2})}
for
n=2
by the reference to the corresponding inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{m}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{1})}\leq 4C_{\mathbb{A}}\left(\|{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{L}_{2\#}}+4C_{\mathbb{A}}\|\mathbf{f}\|^{2}_{L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{-1})}\right).

obtained as inequality (59) in our paper
[
24
]
.
∎

The following assertion is proved similar to Corollary
5.9
.

COROLLARY 5.14
.

Let
T>0
and
n=2
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}([0,T];C^{\infty}_{\#})
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
is such that

{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
and

p\in L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

The next three assertions on spatial-temporal regularity for
n=2
are the corresponding counterparts of Theorems
5.10
,
5.11
and Corollary
5.12
and are proved in a similar way after replacing there
T_{*}
by
T
.

THEOREM 5.15
.

Let
T>0
,
n\geq 2
and
r>0
.
Let
a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in L_{\infty}([0,T];H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1})
,

\tilde{\sigma}>|r-1|+1
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
{\mathbf{f}}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1})

and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(
2.3
)
obtained in Theorem
2.4
is of Serrin-type and
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2})\cup L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-1})
,

p\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r})
.

THEOREM 5.16
.

Let
T>0
,
n=2
and
r>0
.
Let

a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}([0,T];C^{\infty}_{\#})
,
and the relaxed ellipticity condition (
1.4
) hold.
Let
k\in[1,r+1)
be an integer.
Let

{\mathbf{f}}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-2-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})
,
l=0,1,\ldots,k-1
,
and
\mathbf{u}^{0}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes
initial value problem (
2.1
)–(

)
obtained in Theorem

is of Serrin-type and
is such that

\mathbf{u}^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r+1-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k
;

p^{(l)}\in L_{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#}^{r-1-2l})\cap L_{2}(0,T;\dot{H}_{\#\sigma}^{r-2l})
,
l=0,\ldots,k-1
.

.

Let

and

.

,

) hold.

,
and

.

Then the solution
\mathbf{u}
of the anisotropic Navier-Stokes

)–(

)
obtained in Theorem

is of Serrin-type and
is such that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty})
,

p\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{\#}^{\infty})
.

6
Auxiliary results

6.1
Advection term properties

The divergence theorem and periodicity
imply the following identity for any
{\mathbf{v}}_{1},{\mathbf{v}}_{2},{\mathbf{v}}_{3}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#}
.

\displaystyle\left\langle({\mathbf{v}}_{1}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{v}}_{2},{\mathbf{v}}_{3}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\nabla\cdot\left({\mathbf{v}}_{1}({\mathbf{v}}_{2}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{3})\right)d{\mathbf{x}}-\left\langle(\nabla\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}){\mathbf{v}}_{3}+({\mathbf{v}}_{1}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{v}}_{3},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle({\mathbf{v}}_{1}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{v}}_{3},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}-\left\langle(\nabla\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}){\mathbf{v}}_{3},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}

(6.1)

Hence for any
{\mathbf{v}}_{1},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#}
,

\left\langle({\mathbf{v}}_{1}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{v}}_{2},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle(\nabla\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}){\mathbf{v}}_{2},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}=-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\rm div}\,{\mathbf{v}}_{1},|{\mathbf{v}}_{2}|^{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}.

(6.2)

In view of (
6.1
) we obtain the identity

\displaystyle\left\langle({\mathbf{v}}_{1}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{v}}_{2},{\mathbf{v}}_{3}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}

\displaystyle\!\!=\!-\left\langle({\mathbf{v}}_{1}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{v}}_{3},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\quad\forall\ {\mathbf{v}}_{1}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#\sigma},\ {\mathbf{v}}_{2},\,{\mathbf{v}}_{3}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#}\,,

(6.3)

and hence the following well known formula for any
{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#\sigma}
,

{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#}
,

\left\langle({\mathbf{v}}_{1}\cdot\nabla){\mathbf{v}}_{2},{\mathbf{v}}_{2}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}=0.

(6.4)

Equations (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) evidently hold also for
{\mathbf{v}}_{1}
,
{\mathbf{v}}_{2}
, and
{\mathbf{v}}_{3}
from the more general spaces, for which the dual products in (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) are bounded and in which
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#\sigma}
and
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{\#}
, respectively, are densely embedded.

6.2
Some point-wise multiplication results

Let us accommodated to the periodic function spaces in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
n\geq 1
, a particular case of a much more general Theorem 1 in Section 4.6.1 of
[
28
]
about point-wise products of functions/distributions.

THEOREM 6.1
.

Assume
n\geq 1
,
s_{1}\leq s_{2}
and
s_{1}+s_{2}>0
.
Then there exists a constant
C_{*}(s_{1},s_{2},n)>0
such that for any
f_{1}\in H^{s_{1}}_{\#}
and
f_{2}\in H^{s_{1}}_{\#}
,

(a)
f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\in H^{s_{1}}_{\#}\text{ and }\|f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{\#}}\leq C_{*}(s_{1},s_{2},n)\|f_{1}\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{\#}}\,\|f_{2}\|_{H^{s_{2}}_{\#}}\quad\text{if }s_{2}>n/2;

(b)
f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\in H^{s_{1}+s_{2}-n/2}_{\#}\text{ and }\|f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\|_{H^{s_{1}+s_{2}-n/2}_{\#}}\leq C_{*}(s_{1},s_{2},n)\|f_{1}\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{\#}}\,\|f_{2}\|_{H^{s_{2}}_{\#}}\quad\text{if }s_{2}<n/2.

Proof.

Items (a) and (b) follow, respectively, from items (i) and (iii) of
[
28
, Theorem 1 in Section 4.6.1]
when we take into account the norm equivalence in the standard and periodic Sobolev spaces.
∎

Let

(u\star v)(\boldsymbol{\xi}):=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}u(\boldsymbol{\eta})v(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}),\quad\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},

be the convolution in
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.
We will need the following Young’s type inequality for discrete convolution of sequences in
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. For other choices of parameters see, e.g.,
[
6
, p. 316]
and references therein.

LEMMA 6.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
1\leq q\leq\infty

Then the convolution the sequences
u\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})
and
v\in\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})
belongs to
\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})
and

\displaystyle\|u\star v\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})}\leq\|u\|_{\ell_{1}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})}\|v\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})}.

(6.5)

Proof.

By the triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u\star v\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})}=\left\|\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}u(\boldsymbol{\eta})v(\cdot-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right\|_{\ell_{q}}\leq\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\|u(\boldsymbol{\eta})v(\cdot-\boldsymbol{\eta})\|_{\ell_{q}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}|u(\boldsymbol{\eta})|\|v\|_{\ell_{q}}=\|u\|_{\ell_{1}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})}\|v\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{n})}.

∎

THEOREM 6.3
.

Assume
n\geq 1
.
Let
s,\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
w\in H_{\#}^{s}
,
g\in H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}
,

\tilde{\sigma}>\max\{|s|,|s-\theta+1|\}+\frac{n}{2}=|s-\frac{\theta-1}{2}|+|\frac{\theta-1}{2}|+\frac{n}{2}
.
Then
\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}(gw)-g\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}w\in{H_{\#}^{s-\theta+1}}
and

\|\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}(gw)-g\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}w\|_{H_{\#}^{s-\theta+1}}\leq\bar{C}_{s,\theta,\tilde{\sigma}}|g|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}}\|w\|_{H_{\#}^{s}},

where the constant
\bar{C}_{s,\theta,\tilde{\sigma}}
does not depend on
g
or
w
but may depend on
s
,
\theta
and
\tilde{\sigma}
.

Proof.

By (
1.14
) and (
1.8
) we have,

\displaystyle K(\boldsymbol{\xi}):={\cal F}_{\mathbb{T}}[\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}(gw)-g\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}w](\boldsymbol{\xi})

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{\theta}(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2})^{\theta/2}(\widehat{g}\star\widehat{w})(\boldsymbol{\xi})-(\widehat{g}\star{\cal F}_{\mathbb{T}}[\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}w])(\boldsymbol{\xi})

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{\theta}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}[(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2})^{\theta/2}-(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}|^{2})^{\theta/2}]\widehat{g}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{w}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{2}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}[(\boldsymbol{\eta}\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})]f_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{g}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{w}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{i}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\widehat{\nabla g}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\cdot(\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})f_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{w}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}).

Here

f_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}):=(2\pi)^{\theta-2}\frac{(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2})^{\theta/2}-(1+|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}|^{2})^{\theta/2}}{|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}|^{2}}=\frac{\varrho^{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi})-\varrho^{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})}{\varrho^{2}(\boldsymbol{\xi})-\varrho^{2}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})},

and we took into account that

|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{2}-|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}|^{2}=\boldsymbol{\eta}\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\eta}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})
.
Since the inequality

|c_{1}^{\beta}-c_{2}^{\beta}|\leq

|\beta||c_{1}-c_{2}|(c_{1}^{\beta-1}+c_{2}^{\beta-1})
holds for any
c_{1},c_{2}>0
,

\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have,

|f_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|\leq\frac{|\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi})+\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|}{|\varrho(\boldsymbol{\xi})+\varrho(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|}.

Hence

\displaystyle 2\pi|(\boldsymbol{\xi}+\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})f_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi(|\boldsymbol{\xi}|+|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}|)|f_{\theta}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi|\theta|\left[\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi})+\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right]\frac{|\boldsymbol{\xi}|+|\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta}|}{\varrho(\boldsymbol{\xi})+\varrho(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})}\leq|\theta|\left[\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi})+\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right],

for any
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then

\displaystyle|K(\boldsymbol{\xi})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{|\theta|}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left[\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi})+\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\right]|\widehat{\nabla g}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{w}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{|\theta|}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left[\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi})|\widehat{\nabla g}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{w}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|+|\widehat{\nabla g}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\widehat{w}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{|\theta|}\left[\varrho^{\theta-1}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\{|\widehat{\nabla g}|\star|\widehat{w}|\}(\boldsymbol{\xi})+\{|\widehat{\nabla g}|\star|\varrho^{\theta-1}\widehat{w}|\}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\right].

Taking into account Petree’s inequality

\varrho^{s}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\leq\frac{2^{|s|/2}}{(2\pi)^{|s|}}\varrho^{|s|}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\varrho^{s}(\boldsymbol{\xi}-\boldsymbol{\eta})\ \forall\ \boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},\ \forall\ s\in{\mathbb{R}},

and the discrete Young’s type inequality (
6.5
) for convolutions with
q=2
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}(gw)-g\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}w\|_{H_{\#}^{s-\theta+1}}

\displaystyle=\|\varrho^{s-\theta+1}K\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}\leq{|\theta|}\left\|\varrho^{s}\{|\widehat{\nabla g}|\star|\widehat{w}|\}+\varrho^{s-\theta+1}\{|\widehat{\nabla g}|\star|\varrho^{\theta-1}\widehat{w}|\}\right\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{|s|/2}|\theta|}{(2\pi)^{|s|}}\left\||\varrho^{|s|}\widehat{\nabla g}|\star|\varrho^{s}\widehat{w}|+|\varrho^{|s-\theta+1|}\widehat{\nabla g}|\star|\varrho^{s}\widehat{w}|\right\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{|s|/2}|\theta|}{2\pi}\left(\|\varrho^{|s|}\widehat{\nabla g}\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}+\|\varrho^{|s-\theta+1|}\widehat{\nabla g}\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}\right)\|\varrho^{s}\widehat{w}\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}.

By the Schwartz inequality for any
\tilde{\sigma}>|s|+n/2
we have,

\displaystyle\|\varrho^{|s|}\widehat{\nabla g}\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\varrho^{|s|}(\boldsymbol{\xi})|\widehat{\nabla g}(\boldsymbol{\xi})|\leq\left[\sum_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\varrho^{2\tilde{\sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\xi})|\widehat{\nabla g}(\boldsymbol{\xi})|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\left[\sum_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\varrho^{2|s|-2\tilde{\sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\right]^{1/2}

(6.6)

Similarly, for any
\tilde{\sigma}>|s-\theta+1|+n/2
we have,

\displaystyle\|\varrho^{|s-\theta+1|}\widehat{\nabla g}\|_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}\leq\left[\sum_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\varrho^{2\tilde{\sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\xi})|\widehat{\nabla g}(\boldsymbol{\xi})|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\left[\sum_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\varrho^{2|s-\theta+1|-2\tilde{\sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\right]^{1/2}

(6.7)

Then for

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}>\tilde{\sigma}_{0}:=\max\{|s|+\frac{n}{2},|s-\theta+1|+\frac{n}{2}\}=\left|s-\frac{\theta-1}{2}\right|+\left|\frac{\theta-1}{2}\right|+\frac{n}{2}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}(gw)-g\Lambda_{\#}^{\theta}w\|_{H_{\#}^{s-\theta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}_{s,\theta,\tilde{\sigma}}\|{\nabla g}\|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}\|\widehat{w}\|_{H_{\#}^{s}}\leq\bar{C}_{s,\theta,\tilde{\sigma}}|{g}|_{H_{\#}^{\tilde{\sigma}+1}}\|\widehat{w}\|_{H_{\#}^{s}},

where

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{s,\theta,\tilde{\sigma}}:=\frac{2^{|s|/2}}{2\pi}|\theta|\left[\sum_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\varrho^{2\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-n-2\tilde{\sigma}}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\right]^{1/2}.

and (
1.19
) is taken into account.
∎

6.3
Spectrum of the periodic Bessel potential operator

In this section we assume that vector-functions/distributions
\mathbf{u}
are generally complex-valued and the Sobolev spaces
\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{s}
are complex.
Let us recall the definition

\displaystyle\left(\Lambda_{\#}^{r}\,{\mathbf{u}}\right)(\mathbf{x}):=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}}\varrho(\boldsymbol{\xi})^{r}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}(\boldsymbol{\xi})e^{2\pi i\mathbf{x}\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}}\quad\forall\,\mathbf{u}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{s},\ s,r\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(6.8)

of the continuous periodic Bessel potential operator

\Lambda_{\#}^{r}:\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{s}\to\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{s-r}
,
r\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
see (
1.14
), (
1.18
), (
1.22
).

The following assertion is given in
[
24
, Theorem 4 and Remark 2]
.

THEOREM 6.4
.

Let
r\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
r\neq 0
.

(i) Then the operator
\Lambda^{r}_{\#}
in
\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0}
possesses a (non-strictly) monotone sequence of real eigenvalues
\lambda^{(r)}_{j}=\lambda^{r}_{j}
and a real orthonormal sequence of associated eigenfunctions
\mathbf{w}_{j}
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda^{r}_{\#}\mathbf{w}_{j}=\lambda^{r}_{j}\mathbf{w}_{j},\ j\geq 1,\ \lambda_{j}>0,

(6.9)

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\rightarrow+\infty,\ j\rightarrow+\infty;

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j}\in\dot{\mathbf{C}}_{\#\sigma}^{\infty},\quad(\mathbf{w}_{j},\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0}}=\delta_{jk}\quad\forall\,j,k>0.

(6.11)

(ii) Moreover, the sequence
\{\mathbf{w}_{j}\}
is an orthonormal basis in
\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0}
, that is

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w}_{j}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}}\mathbf{w}_{j}

(6.12)

where the series converges in
\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0}
for any
\mathbf{u}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{0}
.

(iii) In addition, the sequence
\{\mathbf{w}_{j}\}
is also an orthogonal basis in
\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
with

(\mathbf{w}_{j},\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}}=\lambda_{j}^{r}\lambda_{k}^{r}\delta_{jk}\quad\forall\,j,k>0.

and for any
\mathbf{u}\in\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
series (
6.12
) converges also in
\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
, that is, the sequence of partial sums

\displaystyle P_{m}\mathbf{u}:=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w}_{j}\rangle_{\mathbb{T}}\mathbf{w}_{j}

(6.13)

converges to
\mathbf{u}
in
\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#\sigma}^{r}
as
m\to\infty
.
The operator
P_{m}
defined by (
6.13
) is for any
r\in{\mathbb{R}}
the orthogonal projector operator from
{\mathbf{H}}^{r}_{\#}
to
{\rm Span}\{\mathbf{w}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{w}_{m}\}
.

6.4
Isomorphism of divergence and gradient operators in periodic spaces

The following assertion proved in
[
24
, Lemma 2]
provides for arbitrary
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
and dimension
n\geq 2
the periodic version of Bogovskii/deRham–type results well known for non-periodic domains and particular values of
s
, see, e.g.,
[
5
]
,
[
3
]
and references therein.

LEMMA 6.5
.

Let
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
n\geq 2
.
The following operators are isomorphisms,

\displaystyle{\rm div}\,:\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#g}^{s+1}\to\dot{H}_{\#}^{s},

(6.14)

\displaystyle{\rm grad}\,:\dot{H}_{\#}^{s}\to\dot{\mathbf{H}}_{\#g}^{s-1}.

(6.15)

6.5
Some functional analysis results

Let us provide the Sobolev embedding theorem that can be considered, e.g., as a particular case of
[
28
, Section 2.2.4, Corollary 2]
adapted to the periodic spaces.

THEOREM 6.6
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
be the dimension,
q_{0}<q_{1}<\infty
and
q_{1}\geq 1
. The periodic Bessel-potential space
H^{s}_{\#q}
is continuously embedded in
L_{\#q_{1}}
if and only if
\displaystyle\frac{s}{n}\geq\frac{1}{q_{0}}-\frac{1}{q_{1}}.

The following version of the Sobolev interpolation inequality without a multiplicative constant, generalised also to any real (including negative) smoothness indices, on periodic Bessel-potential spaces was given in
[
24
, Theorem 5]
.

THEOREM 6.7
.

Let
s
,
s_{1}
,
s_{2}
,
\theta_{1}
,
\theta_{2}
be real numbers such that
0\leq\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq 1
,
\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}=1
and

s=\theta_{1}s_{1}+\theta_{2}s_{2}
.
Then for any
g\in H_{\#}^{s_{1}}\cap H_{\#}^{s_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\|g\|_{H_{\#}^{s}}\leq\|g\|_{H_{\#}^{s_{1}}}^{\theta_{1}}\|g\|_{H_{\#}^{s_{2}}}^{\theta_{2}}.

(6.16)

Theorem 3.1 and Remark 3.2 in Chapter 1 of
[
19
]
imply the following assertion.

THEOREM 6.8
.

Let
X
and
Y
be separable Hilbert spaces and
X\subset Y
with continuous injection.
Let
u\in W^{1}(0,T;X,Y)
.
Than
u
almost everywhere on
[0,T]
equals to a function

\tilde{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];Z)
, where
Z=[X,Y]_{1/2}
is the intermediate space.
Moreover, the trace
u(0)\in Z
is well defined as the corresponding value of
\tilde{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];Z)
at
t=0
.

The following assertion was proved in
[
24
, Lemma 4]
.

LEMMA 6.9
.

Let
s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
s^{\prime}\leq s
and
u\in W^{1}(0,T;H^{s}_{\#},H^{s^{\prime}}_{\#})
be real-valued.

(i) Then

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|u\|^{2}_{H^{(s+s^{\prime})/2}_{\#}}=2\langle\Lambda_{\#}^{s^{\prime}}u^{\prime},\Lambda_{\#}^{s}u\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}=2\langle\Lambda_{\#}^{s^{\prime}+s}u^{\prime},u\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}

(6.17)

for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
and also in the distribution sense on
t\in(0,T)
.

(ii) Moreover, for any real-valued
v\in W^{1}(0,T;{H}_{\#}^{-s^{\prime}},{H}_{\#}^{-s})
and
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left[\langle{u}^{\prime}(\tau),{v}(\tau)\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}+\langle{u}(\tau),{v}^{\prime}(\tau)\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}\right]d\tau=\left\langle{u}(t),{v}(t)\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}-\left\langle{u}(0),{v}(0)\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{T}}}.

(6.18)

6.6
Gronwall’s inequalities.

Gronwall’s inequality is well known and can be found, e.g., in
[
9
, Appendix B.2.j]
,
[
27
, Lemma A.24]
.
Here we provide its slightly more general version valid also for arbitrary-sign coefficients.

LEMMA 6.10
.

Let
\eta:[0,T]\to{\mathbb{R}}
be an absolutely continuous function that satisfies the differential inequality

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}(t)\leq\phi(t)\eta(t)+\psi(t)\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in[0,T],

(6.19)

where
\phi
and
\psi
are real integrable functions.

(a) Then

\displaystyle\eta(t)\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}\phi(r)dr}[\eta(0)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{0}^{s}\phi(\rho)d\rho}\psi(s)ds]\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

(6.20)

(b) Moreover, for non-negative
\phi
and
\psi
(
6.20
) implies

\displaystyle\eta(t)\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}\phi(r)dr}[\eta(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s)ds]\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

(6.21)

(c) In particular, if
\eta
is non-negative, while
\psi\equiv 0
on
[0,T]
and
\eta(0)=0
, then
\eta\equiv 0
on
[0,T]
.

Proof.

Multiplying (
6.19
) by

\displaystyle a(t):=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\phi(r)dr}>0,

we obtain

\frac{d}{dt}[a(t)\eta(t)]\leq a(t)\psi(t).

Integration gives

a(t)\eta(t)\leq a(0)\eta(0)+\int_{0}^{t}a(s)\psi(s)ds.

Dividing by
a(t)
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\eta(t)\leq\frac{1}{a(t)}[\eta(0)+\int_{0}^{t}a(s)\psi(s)ds]\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T]

giving (
6.20
) and thus proving item (a).
Items (b) and (c) follow from (
6.20
).
∎

Let us slightly generalise and give an alternative proof of

[
27
, Lemma 10.3]
.

LEMMA 6.11
.

Let
\eta:[0,T]\to[0,\infty)
be an absolutely continuous function that satisfies the differential inequality

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}(t)+by(t)\leq cy(t)\eta(t)+\psi(t),\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in[0,T];\quad\eta(0)=\eta_{0},

(6.22)

where
\psi,y\geq 0
are integrable real functions, while
b,c>0
and
\eta_{0}\geq 0
are real constants.

If

\displaystyle D:=\eta_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\psi(\tau)d\tau<\frac{b}{ec}

(6.23)

then

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\eta(\tau)<{D}{e}<\frac{b}{c}\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{0}^{T}y(\tau)d\tau<\frac{1}{c}.

(6.24)

Proof.

By Lemma
6.10
(a), inequality (
6.22
) and condition (
6.23
) lead to

\displaystyle a(t)\eta(t)+b\int_{0}^{t}a(s)y(s)ds\leq\eta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}a(s)\psi(s)ds\leq D,

(6.25)

where
a(s):=e^{-cY(s)}>0
and
Y(s):=\int_{0}^{s}y(\tau)d\tau
.
Inequality (
6.25
) implies

\displaystyle be^{-cY(t)}Y(t)\leq b\int_{0}^{t}a(s)y(s)ds\leq D<\frac{b}{ec}\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

(6.26)

Let us consider the function
f(Y):=e^{-cY}Y
on the interval
0\leq Y<\infty
.
One can elementary obtain that
\max_{0\leq Y<\infty}f(Y)

is reached at
Y=1/c
and equals to
1/(ec)
.
But due to (
6.26
) this maximum for
e^{-cY(t)}Y(t)
is not reached for
t\in[0,T]
and hence
Y(T)<1/c
, giving the second inequality in (
6.24
).

Further, (
6.25
) implies that

\eta(t)\leq\frac{D}{a(t)}=De^{cY(t)}<{D}{e}<\frac{b}{c}\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T],

thus giving the first inequality in (
6.24
).
∎

Let us give a generalisation of
[
27
, Lemma 10.3]

and of Lemma
6.11
.

LEMMA 6.12
.

Let
\eta:[0,T]\to[0,\infty)
be an absolutely continuous function that satisfies the differential inequality

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}(t)+by(t)\leq\left[cy(t)+\phi(t)\right]\eta(t)+\psi(t),\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in[0,T];\quad\eta(0)=\eta_{0},

(6.27)

where
\phi,\psi,y\geq 0
are integrable real functions, while
b,c>0
and
\eta_{0}\geq 0
are real constants.

If

\displaystyle D:=\eta_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\Phi(\tau)}\psi(\tau)d\tau<\frac{b}{c}e^{-1-\Phi(T)},

(6.28)

where
\Phi(s):=\int_{0}^{s}\phi(\tau)d\tau
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\eta(\tau)<\frac{b}{c}\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{0}^{T}y(\tau)d\tau<\frac{1}{c}.

(6.29)

Proof.

By Lemma
6.10
(a), inequality (
6.27
) and condition (
6.28
) lead to

\displaystyle a(t)\eta(t)+b\int_{0}^{t}a(s)y(s)ds\leq\eta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}a(s)\psi(s)ds\leq D,

(6.30)

where
a(s):=e^{-cY(s)-\Phi(s)}>0
and
Y(s):=\int_{0}^{s}y(\tau)d\tau
.
Inequality (
6.30
) implies

\displaystyle be^{-\Phi(T)}e^{-cY(t)}Y(t)\leq b\int_{0}^{t}a(s)y(s)ds\leq D<\frac{b}{c}e^{-1-\Phi(T)}\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

(6.31)

Let us consider the function
f(Y):=e^{-cY}Y
on the interval
0\leq Y<\infty
.
One can elementary obtain that
\max_{0\leq Y<\infty}f(Y)

is reached at
Y=1/c
and equals to
1/(ec)
.
But due to (
6.31
) this maximum of
e^{-cY(t)}Y(t)
is not reached for
t\in[0,T]
and hence
Y(T)<1/c
, giving the second inequality
in (
6.29
).

Further, (
6.30
) implies that

\eta(t)\leq\frac{D}{a(t)}=De^{cY(t)+\Phi(t)}<{D}e^{1+\Phi(T)}<\frac{b}{c}\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T],

thus giving the first inequality
in (
6.29
).
∎

Let us give a version of integral Gronwall’s inequality implied, e.g., by Theorem 1.3 and Remark 1.5 in
[
4
]
.

LEMMA 6.13
.

Let
u
,
b
and
a
be measurable functions in
J=[\alpha,\beta]
, such that
bu,ba\in L_{1}(J)
. Suppose that
b(t)
is non-negative a.e. on
J
.
Suppose

\displaystyle u(t)\leq a(t)+\int_{\alpha}^{t}b(s)u(s)ds,\quad\ \text{a.e. }t\in J.

Then

\displaystyle u(t)\leq a(t)+\int_{\alpha}^{t}a(s)b(s)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}b(\tau)d\tau\right)ds,\quad\ \text{a.e. }t\in J.
